- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 5 Wave 5.10 (system pilot — re-run a Stage 3 manual pilot through the built system, compare results, per `docs/planning/dev-plan.md`'s acceptance rule: "a discrepancy is investigated as a defect in one or the other, never left unexplained") — `docs/pilots/pilot-001-system-rerun.md` re-runs pilot 001 (HD Hyundai / STX Heavy Industries) against the real built system: `bun scripts/co-newbiz/newbiz.ts proc:resolve --procedure brownfield-screening` (matched the manual pilot's 3-step walkthrough exactly) and `bun scripts/co-newbiz/newbiz.ts rag` with only the one dimension the manual pilot had real public data for (Regulatory/Permit, behavioral remedy — matched the manual pilot's Amber grade and rationale exactly). **A real defect was found and documented, not silently absorbed**: the manual pilot explicitly declined to produce an overall RAG grade ("since not all six dimensions are gradable, no overall grade is produced — correctly, per the framework's own rule"), but the system re-run produced and persisted an overall grade (Amber) anyway — `db/lib/rag-engine.ts`'s `assessRun()` (Wave 5.3) defaults every dimension missing from the input scenario to Amber rather than leaving it genuinely unassessed, and always computes/writes an `overall` row from the resulting six (real-or-defaulted) grades. This contradicts the design's own stated rule (`docs/governance/rag-framework.md`, `docs/architecture/ui-spec.md` §3, `skills/rag-scoring-framework/SKILL.md`'s own description) and traces to a real cross-wave gap: the engine (Wave 5.3) was built for a single-call, fully-specified-scenario use case before the web UI (Wave 5.6B) and the RAG-scoring skill (Wave 5.8I) later encoded the incremental, possibly-still-incomplete assessment model the design actually calls for — no prior test exercised the engine with a genuinely partial real-world input, only a fully-specified scenario or the API's empty-state (zero rows), never the CLI's actual default-fill behavior. **Tracked as a follow-up defect, not fixed in this PR** — the pilot re-run's own report documents the recommended fix direction (an explicit per-dimension `assessed` signal in the scenario contract, and skipping the `overall` write entirely rather than defaulting it, until all six dimensions have a real assessment on record) for a dedicated follow-up change, since it touches the engine's core evaluation function and its existing test suite. Full suite **181 pass / 0 fail** (no code change in this PR — pilot re-run plus documentation only). **This completes Stage 5's dev-plan.md wave table (5.1 through 5.10)** — the one open item is the rag-engine defect this pilot surfaced, tracked explicitly rather than left unaddressed.

- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 5 Wave 5.9A (backup/restore/rollback infrastructure — first Wave 5.9 sub-PR, `docs/planning/dev-plan.md`'s "one real rollback rehearsed on a reversible-tagged migration; one real recovery drill executed and logged" acceptance criterion) — `scripts/co-newbiz/backup.ts` (checkpointed SQLite copy + row-count manifest, ADR-0014/0015) and `scripts/co-newbiz/restore.ts` (restore + manifest-verified row-count check, refusing to restore a backup with no manifest). Every existing migration (0001-0009) retroactively tagged `@reversible: true` per ADR-0014's "every migration tagged reversible-or-not at write time" requirement — the actual reversibility mechanism in this codebase is pre-migration backup restore, not a per-migration DOWN script (SQLite + this project's forward-only migration runner). `db/migrations/0010_audit_log_created_at_index.sql` — a real, useful index on `audit_log(created_at DESC, id DESC)` for the admin audit-log viewer's unfiltered chronological scan (`query-performance-tuning`'s own flagged high-traffic pattern), doubling as the migration used to actually rehearse the rollback procedure rather than a synthetic throwaway. **A real defect was found and fixed during the rehearsal itself, not by code review**: the first restore attempt reported "verified" but the migration's effect was still present — `restore.ts` copied the backup's main file over the live path but left the live DB's `-wal` sidecar in place, and SQLite replayed those leftover post-backup WAL frames on next open, silently re-applying exactly the write the restore was meant to undo (the row-count check even passed, because it ran after the replay had already happened). Fixed by checkpoint-truncating the live connection's own WAL in place before closing and copying — re-rehearsed after the fix and confirmed correct. Both drills (recovery: seed → backup → delete → restore → verify content, not just counts; rollback: migrate through 0009 → backup → apply 0010 → confirm index present → restore → confirm index absent and `schema_migrations` back to 9) executed for real against scratch databases and logged in the new `docs/runbooks/backup-restore.md`. **6 tests in `tests/unit/backup-restore.test.ts`**, verify-then-encode (the manual CLI rehearsal ran first, including the failed first attempt, before any assertion was written). `scripts/SCRIPTS.md` gained 2 registry rows. Full suite **181 pass / 0 fail**. Remaining Wave 5.9 scope: broader deployment/runbook documentation and the rest of `docs/security/security-baseline.md`'s pre-deployment gate execution, tracked as a follow-on sub-PR.

- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 5 Wave 5.7A (MCP server — the parallel AI-client surface over the same REST API) — docs/architecture/mcp-integration.md, ADR-0011, design v1.0 §4-11. `mcp/co-newbiz/` follows the `Projects/co-architect/mcp/` pattern (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` + stdio, `index.ts` + `tools/*.ts` + `shared/{logger,errors,rate-limiter,types,rest-client}.ts`). **Non-negotiable rule enforced twice**: no file under `mcp/co-newbiz/` imports `bun:sqlite` or any `db/client`/`db/repositories`/`db/lib` module — every permission check, audit-log entry, and MNPI exclusion happens once, at the REST layer, so no AI client can bypass it through a shorter code path (ADR-0011, FR-SYS-01). This is asserted by a `bun:test` file-scan, not just a design statement. `shared/rest-client.ts` implements mcp-integration §5's retry rule precisely: a persistent 4xx (e.g. a 403 membership/MNPI denial) is a terminal, meaningful response and is never retried — only network failures and 5xx are retried, and never for a write, since retrying a mutation risks a duplicate side effect. Read and write tools get separate token-bucket budgets (60/min vs 10/min). 11 tools registered, scoped to exactly the REST surface Wave 5.6A actually built (api-spec §2/§3/§5/§7): `list_projects`, `get_project_status` (project detail + RAG combined), `get_rag_assessment`, `get_approval_chain`, `get_compliance_status`, `list_waivers`, `search_interpretations`, `get_procedure`, `lookup_term`, `list_notifications`, and the one write tool `manage_watch` (create/delete). `list_projects` never passes `include_mnpi=1`, so MNPI-flagged projects stay excluded by default per FR-SEC-06/FR-SYS-02 — the REST layer's own default already enforces this, the tool simply never opts out. Deferred to a later sub-PR, same reasoning as every prior 5.6 deferral — the backing engines run through the CLI today, not a REST endpoint: `search_series`/`get_observations`, `list_data_changes`, `get_financial_analysis`/`get_peer_benchmark`/`get_projection`, `get_assumptions`/`set_assumption`✱, `get_proxy_chain`/`calibrate_chain`✱, `compare_runs`, `get_value_chain_linkage`, `get_antitrust_assessment`, `get_customer_risk`, `get_incentive_exposure`, `get_compensation_gap`, `get_liability_exposure`, `compare_sites`, `list_partner_candidates`, `list_regulatory_requirements`/`get_permit_path`, `list_dd_findings`, `get_rbd_source_profile`, `run_model`✱, `run_simulation`✱, `add_dd_finding`✱, `generate_report`✱, `export_report`. **16 tests in `tests/unit/mcp.test.ts`**, verify-then-encode (a throwaway `smoke.mcp.ts` script exercised every tool against a real Bun.serve instance of the Hono app first — including catching a false-positive in the DB-import scan regex, which initially matched the rule's own header-comment prose before being narrowed to only match actual `import` statements — before being encoded). One test spins a real `StdioClientTransport`/`Client` pair from the SDK against the actual server binary (`bun run mcp/co-newbiz/index.ts`) and confirms `listTools`/`callTool` work over the real JSON-RPC wire protocol, not just via the internal `dispatch()` function. Also manually verified end-to-end against a real running dev server: a genuine bearer token issued via `POST /api/tokens`, consumed by a real stdio MCP client process, correctly returning the caller's membership-filtered project list. **`docs/architecture/mcp-integration.md §7`'s "at least one real call from Claude Desktop, Antigravity, and Codex CLI" item is explicitly NOT claimed complete here** — that requires live client applications this environment cannot drive; `.mcp.json` registers the server for Claude Code today, and the same stdio command works unmodified for the other three clients per §4's table once someone runs it with a real token. Full suite **175 pass / 0 fail**. `package.json` gained the `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` dependency; `.env.sample` documents `CO_NEWBIZ_API_BASE_URL`/`CO_NEWBIZ_API_TOKEN`.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 5 Wave 5.6B (React SPA — dashboard, project detail, procedure viewer, glossary tooltip, admin console) — docs/architecture/ui-spec.md, design v1.0 §4-10. `app/web/` is a Vite + React 18 + TypeScript project (`bun install && bun run build` → `app/web/dist`, served by `app/server/index.ts` at runtime; `bun run dev` proxies `/api` to the backend on port 8787 for local development). Implements the RAG display contract exactly (`ui-spec §3`): the overall badge never renders a color when any of the six dimensions (financial, market, data confidence, regulatory permit, technical execution, risk) is unassessed — it shows the unassessed placeholder (rendered in Korean in the UI per the UI language policy) instead, and every dimension pairs an icon with its color and label (never color-only, FR-SYS-05) with the rationale line always visible, never hover-only. The procedure viewer renders `proc:resolve`'s merged result with a per-step shared/region/entity source badge (Korean labels in the UI). The glossary tooltip is a real `<button>` (keyboard-reachable, `aria-expanded`/`aria-describedby`) that lazy-loads `/api/glossary/:term` on hover or focus. Tabs are client-side state, not nested routes, so `/projects/:id` never exceeds ui-spec's 3-depth navigation cap regardless of which tab is open. The must-change-password gate has its own forced-redirect screen (`/password-change`), matching the backend's allowlist. Admin console covers the three sections the Wave 5.6A backend actually serves (users, audit log, data sources) — schedule/notification/governance/glossary/master-data admin sections are left as backend follow-ups, not built against endpoints that don't exist yet. Explicitly out of scope for this sub-PR, same reasoning as 5.6A's deferred analysis-run endpoints: financial charts (ROIC waterfall, peer boxplot), the proxy-chain diagram, simulation histogram/CDF, model waterfall/tornado, version-comparison bridge, the regulatory-permit DAG, M&A/R&BD review panels, DD findings, and report generation — none of their backing data exists yet (api-spec §4/§6 are still deferred). **A real routing bug was found and fixed during manual browser verification, not caught by the Wave 5.6A test suite** (which only calls `app.request()` at exact `/api/*` paths): `app/server/index.ts` mounted every route module at `app.route("/", xRoutes)`, and each module's own `.use("*", requireAuth)` therefore became a global middleware for the *entire app*, not just that module's paths — so `GET /`, `GET /login`, and every SPA client-side route hit `requireAuth` and returned 401 before the static-SPA fallback (registered even later) ever had a chance to run. Fixed by moving a single non-`/api/*` static/SPA-shell middleware to the front of the chain, registered before any auth-gated router mounts, so `/api/*` still flows through untouched while every other path is served the SPA shell directly. Verified end-to-end in a real browser against a fresh migrated dev DB: login → forced password-change redirect → project list → project creation → RAG unassessed-placeholder state → glossary tooltip (WACC) → procedure viewer with source badges → admin console (users/audit-log/data-sources), all confirmed via page-text extraction, not just "it built." `.gitignore` gained `*.tsbuildinfo`.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 5 Wave 5.6A (Hono API — auth/tokens/projects/governance/monitoring/glossary/admin console) — docs/architecture/api-spec.md, design v1.0 §4-11. `app/server/index.ts` is the `buildApp()` entrypoint (Bun.serve wired only when `import.meta.main`, so tests exercise routes through `app.request()` with no port bound); serves `app/web/dist` as a static SPA with an index.html fallback once Wave 5.6B produces it. **`app/server/middleware.ts`**: single `requireAuth` gate resolving either a `co_newbiz_session` cookie (full read/write) or an `Authorization: Bearer` API token (scope-limited) into one `AuthContext`, in a fixed order — authenticate → scope → CSRF (SameSite=Lax primary defense, Origin/Host cross-check as belt-and-suspenders) → must-change-password gate (allowlist of exactly `/me`, `/logout`, `/password-change`, so a forced-reset account can do nothing else on either credential path — closing the token side of a gap security-model §4 only covered for sessions). **`db/lib/api-tokens.ts`** (new, migration `0009_api_tokens.sql`): Bearer credentials for MCP/CLI clients, storage rule identical to sessions (FR-SEC-09 — only the SHA-256 of the random 32-byte token is stored, so a DB dump is not a credential set); the plaintext token is returned exactly once, on issue. **Route modules** (`auth.ts`, `tokens.ts`, `projects.ts`, `governance.ts`, `monitoring.ts`, `glossary.ts`, `admin.ts`): every project-scoped read goes through `getProjectIfVisible`/`listVisibleProjects` first, so a non-member gets a bare 404 — never 403 — across projects, RAG, approval-chain, compliance, waivers, and watch endpoints (FR-SEC-01, "existence itself is protected" now holds at the HTTP layer, not just the repository layer). Self-registration always lands as `viewer`/`pending` — no unauthenticated caller can mint analyst/admin. MNPI classification write requires admin AND membership (an admin without membership gets 404 before role is even checked, FR-SEC-02). RAG responses always include each dimension's `rationale` (ui-spec's no-color-only-signal rule, enforced at the response shape, not left to the client). Admin console routes return user/audit/schedule/source/retention surfaces only — never project data — and `audit_log` has no DELETE route at any privilege level, on top of migration 0008's DB-level append-only triggers (FR-SEC-07 enforced twice). Deferred to a later sub-PR per this file's own header comment: api-spec §4 (analysis runs/simulations) and §6 (M&A special-topic reads) — those engines run through the CLI today and need an async job surface, not a synchronous handler; noted so the deferral is a decision, not a silent gap. **39 tests in `tests/unit/api.test.ts`**, verify-then-encode (a throwaway `smoke.api.ts` script exercised all 41 real HTTP flows first — including one genuine fixture bug it caught, an admin data-sources check reading `data[0]` of an empty array before any `md_source` row existed — before being encoded as `bun:test` assertions and deleted). Full suite **159 pass / 0 fail**. `.gitignore` gained `*.sqlite-wal`/`*.sqlite-shm` (scratch test-DB WAL/SHM files were not previously excluded, only the base `.sqlite`).
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 5 Wave 5.5 (authentication, accounts, sessions, audit — **Wave 5.5 complete; the §4-9-1 information-protection core is now fully enforced in schema + engine + repository**) — design v1.0 §4-9/§4-9-1, docs/security/security-model.md §4-7, ADR-0009/0012/0038/0039/0040, FR-SEC-01..09. Acceptance gate was security-model §7's full 8-point "one gap and it fails" list, verified point by point against real code before encoding. `db/migrations/0008_accounts_and_pii.sql`: `app_user` (pending→active lifecycle, lockout columns, `wall_group`), `password_history`, `session` (only the SHA-256 of the 32-byte bearer token is stored — a DB dump cannot be replayed as a login), plus the PII tables the schema itself protects: `comp_benchmark` with `CHECK (headcount >= 3)` (the k=3 aggregate floor — a 1-2 person row re-identifies members even without a name column, so individual-granularity pay data is rejected *by the schema*, security-model §7 item 5) and `management_package.executive_ref` with a byte-based GLOB surrogate-key CHECK (a Korean real name fails the `[A-Z]` start, "John Smith" fails the no-space/no-lowercase rule, `TARGET-CEO-01` passes — §7 item 6). `v_comp_anonymized` drops both payroll-median columns. Two `BEFORE DELETE/UPDATE` triggers abort any audit_log mutation with `RAISE(ABORT, 'audit_log is append-only (FR-SEC-07, ADR-0012)')` — §7 item 7 now holds at the DB level, closing the gap migration 0002's comment deferred to deployment config. **`db/lib/accounts.ts`**: full auth lifecycle importing `hashPassword`/`verifyPassword` from `scripts/lib/auth.ts` (FR-SEC-09 requires REUSE, not a copy — PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 / 210,000 iter / 16B salt / 32B key): pending→approve, authenticate (unknown user and wrong password both return `invalid_credentials` so existence is never leaked; 5 consecutive failures → 30-minute lock with the 5th failure itself audited; success clears the counter and unlocks an expired lock), 12-hour sessions with revoke-all semantics, `changePassword` (verifies current, blocks reuse against the current hash *and every password_history row*, archives the old hash, revokes all sessions, issues a fresh one — the completion step of the forced must-change flow), and `resetPassword` (admin sets a temp password, `must_change_password=1`, revokes every session, and refuses a disabled account outright — re-enabling is a separate audited decision). Verified 15/15 by live engine runs including the dump checks: no plaintext password and no plaintext session token anywhere in a full DB dump (§7 item 8). **`db/lib/access-control.ts`**: the single membership write path — `grantProjectMembership` refuses an empty/whitespace `need_to_know_basis` (design §4-9-1(a): membership IS the access decision, so its justification can never be optional) and refuses a grantee with no `app_user` row (FR-SEC-08: agents have no accounts, no phantom members); on an `mnpi_flag=1` project the grantee lands on `insider_list` in the same transaction, so no state exists where an MNPI project member is absent from its insider roster; `revokeProjectMembership` closes the insider row and strips access in one transaction; `appendAudit`/`logDataAccess` are the shared writers every security event funnels through. **`db/repositories/project.repository.ts` 1.0.0 → 1.1.0**: every read now also enforces the wall_group exclusion (FR-SEC-04 — an information wall overrides membership: `app_user.wall_group = project_classification.wall_group` ⇒ invisible, membership row or not; LEFT JOIN so callers without an app_user row are still membership-gated, not silently blocked) and MNPI read logging at the enforcement layer itself (every read returning an `mnpi_flag=1` project writes `data_access_log` — "every access logged" holds because the layer logs, not because callers remember). `VisibilityOptions.excludeMnpi` is the flag the future MCP layer passes `true` by default (FR-SYS-02), and `mnpiScopeOverrideBy` turns the audited scoped-token override into an `mnpi_scope_override` audit row. Membership writes were REMOVED from the repository — exactly one write path exists. **`scripts/co-newbiz/newbiz.ts` 1.8.0 → 1.9.0**: `user:create`/`user:approve`/`user:list`/`user:reset-password`, with passwords read from stdin only, never argv (a shell history or process listing must not capture them, FR-SEC-09); `user:list` emits identity columns only — no hash material ever leaves the engine. Verified end-to-end: fresh migrate (×8), stdin-piped create → approve → reset → list, and duplicate-userid rejection. **41 tests in `tests/unit/security.test.ts`** — one describe per security-model §7 item 1-8, plus wall-group, grant-guard, lockout, session, and password-change/reset flows, all verify-then-encode (every figure first produced by real engine/SQL runs, including the CHECK-constraint rejections and the DB-dump scans). Two test-infrastructure fixes along the way: `observation.repository.test.ts`'s `ORDER BY detected_at` was unstable when two change-log rows land in the same millisecond (tie now broken by `change_type`), and the scratch-DB cleanup retries on Windows where the WAL checkpoint briefly holds the file. Full suite **120 pass / 0 fail**.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 5 Wave 5.4 (monitoring, auto-reanalysis, scheduler — **Wave 5.4 complete**) — design v1.0 §4-3, ADR-0019, FR-MON-01..04. `db/migrations/0007_watch_and_notification.sql`: `schedule_job`/`job_run` (the OS-level scheduler's audit trail, FR-MON-01/04), `watch`/`watch_rule` (per-project subscriptions; `watch.user_id` is a plain identifier, not an FK — no accounts table until Wave 5.5), `notification` (`bundle` column records immediate-vs-daily-digest intent; actual email/webhook transport is out of scope until Wave 5.6/5.9, this wave only decides whether/how a notification should be recorded). **`db/lib/watch-engine.ts`**: `evaluateTransitionNotifications` derives upgrade/downgrade **from the grade-rank comparison itself** (green=2>amber=1>red=0), never from a caller-supplied label, and fires both directions equally — FR-MON-02's non-negotiable rule that a downgrade alert is never optional. Per-(project, trigger_type) cooldown suppresses an immediate re-fire of the *same* trigger while an independent trigger on the same project still fires (verified: two transitions 1 second apart on the same project — the second `rag_upgrade` is suppressed, but a same-instant `rag_downgrade` still fires, since cooldown keys on `(project_id, trigger_type)`, not `project_id` alone). `kill_criteria` bypasses cooldown entirely and always fires at `urgent`/`immediate`/`all` — the same "never delay a danger signal" stance the RAG engine already takes for a Red candidate (Wave 5.3). A successful notification flips the originating `rag_transition.notified` flag (the column Wave 5.3 defined but left unconsumed). **`scripts/co-newbiz/watch-run.ts`** (`watch:run [--rescenarios-dir <dir>] [--db-path]`, wired into `newbiz.ts` 1.7.0 → 1.8.0): the change-detection step is honestly scoped to what Wave 5.1/5.2 actually built — it reads `data_change_log` rows already populated by `ensure_fresh()`/tests (there is no live `fetch` CLI yet) and matches them against a project's `assumption(kind='live', anchor_ref=<series_id>)` rows on its latest run; a project with no matching change is skipped outright, never re-run "just in case" (the design's own rule). Since the full `model` command (assembling a financial model from live data) does not exist yet either, the auto re-run step re-invokes the RAG assessment via a refactored `assessScenario()` (extracted from `rag.ts` so both the CLI and the watch cycle share one persistence path) fed by a caller-supplied updated scenario, labeled `auto ...` / `created_by: watch:run` so an auto-triggered run is distinguishable from a manual one. Each cycle is wrapped in a `job_run` row (`running` → `success`/`failed`+error) per FR-MON-04. Per-watch `last_checked_at` uses the *earliest* watermark across a project's watches (so a newer subscriber never misses a change an older one already consumed) and advances to "now" after every check regardless of outcome. **Verified end-to-end exactly per design §12 item 17**: baseline Amber assessment → an oil-price series revision lands in `data_change_log` → `watch:run` with an updated (improved) scenario detects the change, re-assesses, promotes to Green (hysteresis bar+sustain+7-day hold all satisfied), and records one `rag_upgrade`/`opportunity` notification; an immediate second cycle at the same watermark makes no further change (cooldown-safe by construction, since nothing new to detect). 9 tests across `tests/unit/watch-engine.test.ts` (6, engine-level) and `tests/unit/watch-run.test.ts` (3, full-cycle integration), full suite **80 pass / 0 fail**.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 5 Wave 5.3 (governance engines — **Wave 5.3 complete**) — the three engines that turn the governance design into executable rules, per design v1.0 §4-2/§2-3/§2-5, ADR-0016/0028/0029/0036/0037, FR-RAG-01..08, FR-GOV-04..12. `db/migrations/0006_governance_engines.sql`: `governance_doc` (lands here, not the policy wave, because waiver/interpretation/rag_rule all FK-anchor to it) + nine engine tables (`rag_rule`/`rag_assessment`/`rag_transition`, `approval_chain`/`group_agenda`, `compliance_check`/`waiver`/`interpretation`), all CHECK-constrained, with `waiver.expires_at NOT NULL` at the schema level (ADR-0036 — an indefinite waiver is a de facto repeal) and `remedy_shape` allowed only on `regulatory_permit` rows (FR-RAG-05). **`db/lib/rag-engine.ts`**: priority-ordered overall grading (never weighted average), the data-confidence cap (chain B → Amber ceiling, chain C/staleness → Red "judgment withheld" — verified: an all-six-green input with chain C grades overall Red while its financial dimension stays Green), D-M3-1 financial bands (+2.0pp AND DSCR ≥1.3x Green / below-hurdle Red), FR-RAG-05 remedy shapes (behavioral = Amber for the obligation duration, structural = Amber until discharge then re-gradable Green, prohibited = Red), rag-framework §5 Screening/Pre-FS reduced check (financial amber-capped pending a completed model), and D-M3-4 hysteresis — promote needs bar + sustain across a full run cycle + 7-day hold, demote below +0.5pp with hold, Red candidates immediate, and **recovery out of Red is staged** (a same-day Red→Green flip is exactly the flapping D-M3-4 exists to stop; red→green records Amber after the hold, never one-step Green). Two defects caught during verification and fixed: `assessRun` had a hardcoded `previousIrrOverHurdlePp = null` that made promotion through the persisted path structurally unsatisfiable (now a threaded option, defaulting conservative), and the financial-band margin is now rounded to 1e-6pp because `(0.12-0.10)*100 = 1.9999…pp` in IEEE-754 pushed a mathematically-on-the-band input off the Green band. **`db/lib/approval-chain.ts`**: ADR-0028 hard gate (group entry blocked with `blocked_reason` recorded until entity IC decides; withdrawn_pending_resubmission blocks — pilot 003 F-2, withdrawal ≠ approval), agenda submission gated on the same precondition, and ADR-0029 `rankGroupAgenda` (Red last regardless of IRR, then IRR-over-hurdle desc, then capital desc — verified on a 4-project cycle where the largest-capital Red proposal ranks 4th). **`db/lib/compliance.ts`**: ADR-0037 gate semantics (mandatory-unsatisfied → blocked, recommended-unsatisfied → `reason_required` never blocks — the §2-0 five-layer enforceability rule), the BF six-validator rule carries `waiverEligible=false` at the requirement level (no waiver path), active-waiver lookup is date-authoritative over the status column (an expired 'active' waiver does not unblock — verified), `issueWaiver` refuses a missing expiry outright, waiver concentration ≥3 on one clause is an amendment trigger not a compliance failure (FR-GOV-11), and the interpretation registry enforces explicit same-clause supersession (FR-GOV-12; cross-clause supersession refused with a rationale citing the rule). CLI: `scripts/co-newbiz/rag.ts` (`rag <scenario-json-path> [--db-path]`, wired into `newbiz.ts` 1.6.0 → 1.7.0) creates a `run` row keyed by the scenario's SHA-256 `input_hash` before assessing — design §12 item 14's cap check now runs end-to-end through the real CLI. **29 tests in `tests/unit/governance-engines.test.ts`** (verify-then-encode; all figures produced by real engine runs first), full suite **71 pass / 0 fail**; scratch-DB pattern with WAL cleanup, and the suite runs through `getDb` so `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` is actually enforced (which is how a misleading `entityIc` option was caught — `group_agenda.entity_id` is a real FK).

The actual network fetcher (`scripts/lib/ssrf.ts`'s `safeFetch()`, already present in this scaffold) is dependency-injected rather than imported directly, since no real data-source API keys are configured yet — this keeps the function fully testable now without faking a live external call. Verified with a real, formal test suite (not just an ephemeral script): `tests/unit/observation.repository.test.ts`, 5 tests against actual SQL execution on a scratch DB (deleted after), covering first-fetch, cache-hit, unchanged-value no-op, changed-value new-revision-with-old-preserved, and change-log completeness — all 5 pass.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 5 Wave 5.2a (CLI track, part 1) — `proc:resolve`, the merge engine for `procedures/`'s shared/region/entity `references:`+`overrides:` model (design §6.3, ADR-0020). `scripts/co-newbiz/proc-resolve.ts` implements `add_steps`/`replace_steps`/`thresholds` overrides with strict `_shared → region → entity` precedence and per-step `_source` provenance tagging (feeds the future Procedure Viewer's per-step source badges, `docs/architecture/ui-spec.md` §4). Wired into `newbiz.ts` (1.0.0 → 1.1.0) as `proc:resolve --procedure <name> [--entity <code>] [--region <code>] [--json]`. **Verified against the real procedure files already committed in Stage 2**, not synthetic fixtures — resolving `brownfield-screening` with `--entity ent-a --region EU` produces the correct 5-step merge (ent-a's prescreen step first, the shared 3 steps in order, EU's works-council step correctly inserted after step 2). This surfaced and fixed a real bug during verification: `ent-a`'s `add_steps` entry uses `after: 0` as a documented sentinel meaning "insert at the very beginning" (its own YAML comment says so), but the first implementation treated `after` as always referring to an existing step id, silently appending the step at the end instead of the front — fixed to special-case `after: 0` as the beginning-of-list sentinel unless a real step 0 exists. `scripts/SCRIPTS.md` registered both new scripts.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 5 Wave 5.1 — first real code, DB layer. `db/client.ts` (bun:sqlite connection factory, WAL mode, `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON`, single choke point per ADR-0001/0002), `db/lib/ulid.ts` (26-char Crockford-base32 ULID generator — every table PK, no AUTOINCREMENT), `db/migrate.ts` (pure-SQL migration runner tracked in `schema_migrations`), and two migrations: `0001_master_data.sql` (the 11-table master data catalog from `docs/architecture/db-schema.md` §2, including `staging_unmapped` quarantine) and `0002_project_access_control.sql` (the non-descopable information-protection core — `project_member`, `project_classification`, `insider_list`, `data_access_log`, `audit_log`, per ADR-0038/0039/0040/0012). `db/repositories/project.repository.ts`: the reference implementation of the deny-by-default access pattern (FR-SEC-01/02/03) — every function filters through `project_member`, `getProjectIfVisible` returns `null` (not a permission error) for a non-member so existence itself stays protected, and there is deliberately no admin-bypass variant. Verified end-to-end, not just unit-tested: ran the actual migration against a scratch DB, created a project, confirmed it was invisible to every caller until membership was granted, then confirmed granting membership to one user left it invisible to a second — the core security property `docs/security/security-model.md` §7 item 1 describes, demonstrated against real code for the first time. `scripts/co-newbiz/newbiz.ts` (the design's CLI entrypoint, §4-8) implements `db:migrate` and `status`; remaining commands land with their respective Stage 5 waves rather than being stubbed to silently no-op. `package.json` gained `db:migrate`/`db:status` scripts.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 4 (part 2) — completed the three remaining Stage 4 tasks. `docs/architecture/api-spec.md` (task 4.4): endpoint catalog across 8 domains with the access-control contract stated per endpoint (project-membership filtering, MNPI exclusion, R&BD/rbd-source fields), plus the response-envelope contract that carries every UI source-tooltip requirement directly. `docs/architecture/ui-spec.md` (task 4.4): information architecture, the RAG display contract (no color-only differentiation, no partial-assessment default color, structural-vs-behavioral remedy display), procedure-viewer source-attribution rendering, and the admin console section list. `docs/architecture/mcp-integration.md` (task 4.4): full tool catalog (read-only + write/✱) including a new `get_rbd_source_profile` tool, client registration table, and the REST-only enforcement rule restated as a Stage 5 dependency-graph gate. `docs/security/security-model.md` (task 4.5): trust-boundary diagram, a 14-row STRIDE threat matrix mapping every threat to a specific FR-SEC/FR-DATA requirement, data classification table, and an 8-point "one gap and it fails" verification checklist. `docs/security/security-baseline.md` (task 4.5): recurring security cadence (weekly/monthly/quarterly), pre-deployment gate, vulnerability management, and incident-response procedure. `docs/planning/dev-plan.md` (task 4.6): the Stage 5 wave structure with explicit dependency/parallel-safety table, PR-boundary rules (information protection lands as one unsplittable reviewed unit), per-wave acceptance criteria, and the relationship between wave 5.10's system pilot and the fourteen real cases already walked manually in `docs/pilots/` — 5.10 is a regression check against that existing fixture set, not a new source of findings. **Stage 4 is now complete** — all six tasks (4.1-4.6) landed.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Fourth R&BD pilot — `docs/pilots/pilot-014-c2y-university-techholdings.md`, C2Y, a bio-measurement startup with a real, disclosed investor base including Seoul National University Technology Holdings alongside Woori Bank, Shinhan Capital, and independent VCs, plus an active (not completed) co-research relationship with KRISS (a government-funded research institute) on measurement-standardization reliability. This is the first R&BD case that is not shaped as an acquisition/license/spin-off absorption at all — the reviewing entity's thesis would be a **minority venture investment**, and the target was never anyone's spin-off or licensee, surfacing a genuine scope limit in the R&BD checklist and structure-hypothesis wording. Four real findings applied immediately: `checklists/rbd-technology-checklist.yaml` (1.1.0 → 1.2.0) rbd-02 now requires classifying a government-institute relationship as formal-co-funded vs. informal-collaboration before assessing conditions (the KRISS relationship's grant status isn't public, a real ambiguity this case exposed); rbd-03 extended to cover joint-IP-ownership diligence for active co-research (distinct from a simple license-in ownership chain); rbd-04 clarified to concern the reviewing entity's chosen path (build/license/spin-off/minority-invest), not the target's own founding history; `procedures/_shared/brownfield-pre-fs/schema.yaml` (1.2.0 → 1.3.0) step 2 adds "minority venture investment" as a fourth named R&BD deal shape. `docs/architecture/srs.md` FR-RBD-10/11/12 and `docs/governance/traceability-matrix.md` updated; `docs/pilots/README.md` — fourteen real cases now span ten industries/case-types, with R&BD confirmed across four structurally distinct real shapes (fully-internal spin-off, accelerator-partnered spin-off, external license-in, university-holdco-backed minority investment).
- **[2026-08-20]**: Third R&BD pilot — `docs/pilots/pilot-013-hexapro-etri.md`, Hexapro's real technology-license agreement with ETRI (a government-funded research institute), completing real-case coverage of all three `rbd_source` values (`internal_only`: pilot 011; `accelerator_partnered`: pilot 012; `external_technology_acquisition`: this pilot). Unlike the spin-off pilots, this deal's public disclosure is limited (no contract value, uncertain exact licensed technology) — the pilot treats that reduced disclosure as the central finding rather than filling gaps with inference: license-in deals structurally have a lower public-corroboration ceiling than spin-off announcements, since spin-off news typically carries track-record framing that license announcements don't. Two real findings applied immediately to `checklists/rbd-technology-checklist.yaml` (1.0.0 → 1.1.0): rbd-01 now notes that `external_technology_acquisition` deals should prioritize direct licensor technical documentation over public-source corroboration; rbd-05 broadened from spin-off-shaped wording (equity/brand) to also cover licensor relationships (ongoing royalty obligations, follow-on co-funding program eligibility) — real institutional data captured from ETRI's own "Technology Scale-up R&BD Program" (a real government program whose name uses "R&BD" directly, KRW 360M/project, ≤12 months, ~10 projects/cycle). `docs/architecture/srs.md` FR-RBD-08/09 and `docs/governance/traceability-matrix.md` updated; `docs/pilots/README.md` — thirteen real cases now span ten industries/case-types, with R&BD's `rbd_source` enum fully validated across all three real-case shapes.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Second R&BD pilot — `docs/pilots/pilot-012-lg-studio341.md`, LG Electronics' Studio341 internal-venture program, run jointly with external accelerator Bluepoint Partners (structurally distinct from pilot 011's fully-internal POSCO POVENTURES). Closes a real gap: pilot 011's rbd-02 (government co-funding conditions) was N/A there; this case has real TIPS/DIPS government co-funding program involvement for the spun-off teams, the pilot set's first positive test of that checklist item. Also refines pilot 011's F-2 (internal-vs-external R&BD sub-source distinction) into a three-value `rbd_source` field design (`internal_only` / `accelerator_partnered` / `external_technology_acquisition`), recorded in `docs/architecture/db-schema.md`'s `dd_finding` row and `docs/governance/traceability-matrix.md` §4.5. Real data captured: per-team investment cap (KRW 400M), a ~70% pivot rate during selection (a genuine R&BD technology/commercialization-path risk base rate), and LG's more tightly-coupled post-spin-off synergy-seeking pattern (AI automation/robotics/materials) contrasted with Amberoad's brand-halo-plus-independent-sales pattern. `docs/pilots/README.md` updated — twelve real cases now span ten industries/case-types, with R&BD confirmed to generalize across two structurally distinct real program shapes.
- **[2026-08-20]**: R&BD (Research & Business Development) scope addition, per user decision — treated as a **Brown Field sub-type** (`case_subtype: rbd`), not a new top-level review type alongside GF/BF, since its deal mechanics (equity stake, license-in, spin-off carve-out) are BF-shaped rather than GF site-selection-shaped. Added `checklists/rbd-technology-checklist.yaml` (core-technology TRL distinct from production-process TRL, government co-funding conditions, IP/freedom-to-operate diligence, build/license/spin-off commercialization-path decision with rationale, continuing parent-entity relationship terms, track-record corroboration). Extended `brownfield-pre-fs` (v1.1.0 → v1.2.0) to name "internal-venture spin-off carve-out" as an explicit deal-structure option, and `brownfield-dd` (v1.1.0 → v1.1.0, new step 3, steps renumbered) to run the new checklist for R&BD-flagged deals. Grounded immediately in a real case: `docs/pilots/pilot-011-posco-amberoad.md` — POSCO's internal-venture program (POVENTURES) spinning off Amberoad (2023-09), a manufacturing-AI company with a real pre-spin-off track record (33 internal AI projects, ~KRW 39.7B annual profit impact) and real post-spin-off traction (16 enterprise customers, ~KRW 2B pre-Series A from Stonebridge Ventures/Sparklabs). Every new checklist item and the new structure-hypothesis branch validated cleanly on first real-case use, with one minor refinement flagged (distinguishing internal-spinoff from external-technology-acquisition R&BD sub-sources). `docs/architecture/srs.md` §7.5 and `docs/governance/traceability-matrix.md` §4.5 (new) trace the R&BD requirements; `docs/architecture/db-schema.md`'s `dd_finding` row extended with R&BD-specific fields. `docs/pilots/README.md` updated — eleven real cases now span ten industries/case-types.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 4 (part 1) — began system implementation planning per design v1.0 §10's task table, with Gate 4's three explicit deliverables landed in one pass. `docs/architecture/srs.md` (task 4.1): ~85 functional requirements across 13 domains (data architecture, governance system, RAG engine, monitoring, financial analysis, M&A special topics, Green Field, partnership, information protection, three-stage review, UI/API/MCP, deployment/ops), each traced to a specific governance-artifact clause or pilot finding, prioritized MoSCoW mapped to the existing MVP tiering (ADR-0042: Must=MVP, Should=Tier 2, Could=Tier 3). `docs/governance/traceability-matrix.md` (task 4.2): clause → procedure → requirement → implementation-target → verification mapping for every Must-priority requirement — the mechanism that lets a future `policy:impact` CLI know exactly what to re-check on a regulation amendment, directly closing the design's own problem #9. Several rows cite "already verified" status from Stage 3's ten real-case pilots (e.g. FR-RAG-01's Red-grading path via pilot 010, FR-GF-01's two-stage filter design via pilot 007's contrast case) rather than only forward-looking Stage 5 tests. `docs/architecture/db-schema.md` (task 4.3): full table catalog across 10 domains (master data, fact/observation, assumption/run/versioning, governance, RAG, M&A special topics, Green Field, information protection, review/access), index rationale for every hot-path query, and the ADR-0002 portability constraints restated as a Stage 5 gate checklist. Tasks 4.4 (API/UI/MCP spec), 4.5 (security model), and 4.6 (dev plan) remain open as Stage 4 follow-ups.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 3 (part 10) — pilot 010 (`docs/pilots/pilot-010-affinity-lotte-rental.md`), the rental car industry, per user instruction. Real case: Affinity Equity Partners, already owning Korea's #2 rental car operator (SK Rentcar, acquired 2024 for KRW 820B in a real competitive bid against IMM PE and Glenwood PE), signed to acquire 63.5% of #1 operator Lotte Rental — the Korea Fair Trade Commission **blocked the combination outright** (2026-01-26), the first pilot case with an actual prohibition outcome rather than any form of approval. The real substance was a **common-ownership/common-control** antitrust theory (both rental car majors coming under one PE sponsor) rather than a direct merger of the target entities — a fact pattern `checklists/ma-special-topics-checklist.yaml` and `brownfield-dd` didn't previously name explicitly; both broadened immediately (checklist v1.1.0 → v1.2.0 ma-01, procedure v1.0.0 → v1.1.0 step 3). This is also the first case where the Screening-stage `preliminary_hhi_flags_certain_block` kill criteria would plausibly have fired for real (validating it wasn't dead code) and the first to actually grade Red (not Amber) on the RAG framework's Regulatory/Permit dimension. Real second-order fallout also captured: the prohibition disrupted Lotte Group's separate, contingent hotel-stake-sale financing plans (~KRW 980.5B + ~592.4B), flagged as a minor open finding. `docs/pilots/README.md` updated; pilot 010 §6 notes all four possible antitrust outcome shapes (no remedy, behavioral remedy, structural remedy, prohibition) are now each represented by a real case across the ten-pilot set.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 3 (part 9) — pilots 008 and 009, closing the consumer-industry gap flagged by the user across the prior seven pilots (marine engines, heavy machinery, diversified conglomerate, semiconductors, biopharma, renewable energy — no retail/platform/consumer-services case). Pilot 008 (`docs/pilots/pilot-008-delivery-hero-woowa-brothers.md`): Delivery Hero's real KRW 4.75T acquisition of Woowa Brothers (Baemin), Korea's largest food-delivery platform, approved by the KFTC on 2020-12-28 conditional on divesting Delivery Hero's existing Korean operation (Yogiyo/Baedaltong) — the first **structural** antitrust remedy found in the pilot set (pilot 001 was behavioral, pilot 002 unconditional). Surfaced a genuine RAG-framework gap: the Regulatory/Permit dimension's grading logic never distinguished a one-time closing condition (re-gradable once discharged) from an ongoing behavioral obligation (capped for its full duration) — applied immediately as `docs/governance/rag-framework.md` §2.5 (new). Pilot 009 (`docs/pilots/pilot-009-emart-ebay-korea.md`): Emart's real KRW 3.44T acquisition of 80.01% of eBay Korea via a purpose-built SPC — the richest real financial disclosure of any pilot to date, with both real target operating metrics (GMV/revenue/operating-profit) and real acquirer-side leverage impact (total liabilities KRW 11.8T → 16.2T), still short of a complete DSCR calculation but the closest any pilot has come. Applied F-3 immediately: `brownfield-pre-fs` (v1.0.0 → v1.1.0) step 2 now explicitly prompts for acquisition vehicle (direct/SPC/holdco). `docs/pilots/README.md` updated; nine real cases across eight industries have now been walked with the pattern holding without exception — every finding a real-case-surfaced scope gap, never a procedure logic error.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 3 (part 8) — pilots 006 and 007, sourced from two genuinely different industries per user instruction. Pilot 006 (`docs/pilots/pilot-006-orion-ligachem-bio.md`): Orion's real 25.73% (KRW 548.5B) acquisition of LigaChem Bio, a food/confectionery conglomerate entering ADC biopharma with zero value-chain relationship to its existing business — the first pure-diversification case, surfacing two genuinely new scope gaps only a biopharma target could reveal: the certification-transferability step assumes an operational/manufacturing-type certification and doesn't fit an R&D-stage target's asset-tied regulatory pathway (F-1), and the QoE/normalized-EBITDA step assumes an operating business with historical earnings, which a pre-revenue licensing-model target doesn't have (F-2). Also confirmed the RAG framework's discipline against inferring a Financial grade from a tempting real number (80.1% unrealized stock-value gain) held for a second, unrelated case. Pilot 007 (`docs/pilots/pilot-007-punggu-offshore-wind.md`): the real Punggu offshore wind farm (200MW, Ansan-si/Gyeonggi-do), whose environmental-impact-assessment notification excluded Incheon/Ongjin-gun residents despite their islands sitting within the legally-defined affected-area radius (distance-from-coast, not administrative jurisdiction) — the sharpest, most quantifiable real procedural gap found across all pilots to date, applied immediately to `greenfield-screening` (v1.1.0 → v1.2.0) step 2: the community-notification boundary must now be computed from the applicable legal standard, never assumed equal to administrative jurisdiction. Pilot 007 also supplied the richest real permit-timing calibration data of any pilot (484-day average shared-water-surface permit; 32-month minimum under the new siting regime) and confirmed the design's own two-stage hard-filter-then-weighted-score site-selection model is a deliberate improvement over Korea's real, since-reformed "race to file" wind-measurement-device priority system. `docs/pilots/README.md` updated; pilot 007 §6 extends the cross-pilot synthesis to seven cases across six industries, with the pattern holding: every finding across all seven has been a real-case-surfaced scope gap, never a procedure logic error.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 3 (part 7) — pilot 005 (`docs/pilots/pilot-005-sk-hynix-yongin-cluster.md`), the first Green Field pilot (all of pilots 001-004 were Brown Field/intra-group). Real case: SK hynix's Yongin Semiconductor Cluster (national industrial complex designation 2023-03, ~4.15M m² site, Fab 1 building permit approved and under construction, ~KRW 9.4T Fab 1 facility investment of an estimated ~KRW 31T total), sourced per user instruction to find a real new-business-development case. Walked against all four Green Field procedures (`greenfield-screening`, `greenfield-pre-fs`, `greenfield-fs`, `greenfield-dd`) for the first time. Two strong real matches: infrastructure lead-time/cost mitigation (K-water's dedicated task force and Korea's first integrated raw-to-ultra-pure water supply model; 100%-government-subsidized underground transmission lines) and permit-critical-path-as-schedule-driver (explicit government goal of cutting industrial-complex formation from 10 to 5 years, plus a 2026-08 permit-simplification policy targeting KRW 2.5T of accelerated investment). One structurally significant negative finding (F-1): the real project's infrastructure plans were concretized roughly 2 years *after* site designation — a direct real-world deviation from the procedure's hard-pre-filter-first design intent (meeting M6 D-M6-1) — applied immediately as a new `greenfield-screening` kill-criteria condition (v1.0.0 → v1.1.0), `infrastructure_plan_not_finalized_at_site_announcement` → escalate to risk-officer, since a fabricated case would very likely never have surfaced this real deviation. Also applied F-2 (broadened `checklists/site-selection-checklist.yaml` ss-08 from local-community-only framing to also cover inter-regional resource disputes, v1.0.0 → v1.1.0, same pattern as pilot 002's ma-07/08 fix — this case's real dispute was upstream provinces refusing Han River watershed water supply). `docs/pilots/README.md` updated with resolved/open findings across all five pilots; pilot 005 §7 notes zero findings across all five pilots to date have been a procedure logic error — every one has been a scope gap surfaced specifically because the case was real, not synthetic, which is the strongest evidence yet that ADR-0034's manual-pilot-first principle is working.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 3 (part 6) — closed the Pre-FS/FS/DD/Entity-IC procedure schema gap flagged consistently across all four pilots (F-3/F-5). Added seven new `procedures/_shared/*/schema.yaml` files, GF/BF-split where the design's own process-map differentiates them and shared where it doesn't: `greenfield-pre-fs`, `brownfield-pre-fs` (WACC/hurdle confirmation as a hard precondition, certification-transferability non-inference rule), `greenfield-fs`, `brownfield-fs` (base-rate kill criteria, six-way M&A cross-check start per meeting M5's fixed order), `greenfield-dd`, `brownfield-dd` (the six-validator no-waiver completeness check as an explicit kill criteria condition, referencing the real antitrust risk-shape discrimination validated in pilots 001/002), and `entity-ic-review` (shared for GF/BF). Every gate in the Screening→Group IC sequence now has a formal schema — see `docs/governance/process-map.md` §Gate Procedures for the full table. Both `entity-ic-review` and `group-investment-review` (bumped 1.0.0 → 1.1.0) gained a fourth decision state, `withdrawn_pending_resubmission`, closing pilot 003's finding F-2 (the original three-state approved/rejected/conditional model couldn't represent Doosan Bobcat/Robotics' real withdraw-and-re-propose pattern). `docs/governance/rag-framework.md` §6 updated with pilot 004's finding that calibration has two structurally different paths — public-case backtestable (antitrust-adjacent steps) vs. internal-data-only (group-agenda-ranking's quantitative mechanics), the latter never having a public substitute. `docs/pilots/README.md` now tracks which findings are resolved vs. still open.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 3 (part 5) — ran both pilot 003 and pilot 004 candidates per user instruction. Pilot 003 (`docs/pilots/pilot-003-doosan-bobcat-robotics.md`) walked the real Doosan Bobcat/Doosan Robotics comprehensive share-exchange restructuring (announced 2024-07-11, two FSS securities-filing rejections, board withdrawal 2024-08-29, revised ratio and re-proposal 2024-10-21, Align Partners activist opposition, suspended following the December 2024 martial-law situation) against `procedures/_shared/group-investment-review/schema.yaml` — its intra-group-transaction-compliance step (Fair Trade Act §47 screen) is the strongest single real-data match found across all pilots to date, and a genuine schema gap surfaced (the withdraw-then-resubmit pattern doesn't map onto the schema's approved/rejected/conditional decision states). Pilot 004 (`docs/pilots/pilot-004-sk-group-capital-rebalancing.md`) walked SK Group's 2023-2025 cross-subsidiary capital reallocation (SK hynix's AI/HBM profit swing funding SK Innovation-SK E&S merger and SK On/SK Ecoplant absorptions) against the same procedure's group-agenda-ranking step — validated the ranking logic qualitatively, but found its quantitative mechanics (IRR-over-hurdle spread, capital-requested figures) have **no public-data calibration path at all**, structurally unlike the antitrust-screening steps pilots 001-002 could backtest against real regulatory decisions; noted as a `docs/governance/rag-framework.md` §6 calibration-strategy input. §6 of pilot 004 synthesizes all four pilots to date: every step of both formalized procedures has now been at least partially exercised with zero fabricated data, and the Pre-FS/FS/DD/Entity-IC procedure schema gap (first flagged in pilot 001) remains the one consistently actionable finding, now informed by pilot 003's concrete withdraw-and-resubmit worked example.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 3 (part 4) — researched two more real-case candidates for pilot 003, deliberately outside shipbuilding (per user instruction), targeting two gaps pilots 001/002 couldn't reach: ADR-0029 Group IC ranking and the missing Pre-FS/FS/Entity-IC procedure content. Candidate A (recommended): Doosan Bobcat / Doosan Robotics comprehensive share exchange (2024) — an intra-group (not cross-group) restructuring with a real, dense paper trail (disclosed exchange ratio, two FSS securities-filing rejections, a board withdrawal 49 days after announcement, a revised ratio, and an Align Partners activist shareholder campaign) that directly exercises the design's group-review-agent scope and gate-level valuation-fairness review, though the real-world case is currently suspended following the December 2024 martial-law situation, not concluded. Candidate B (reserve): SK Group's 2023-2025 cross-subsidiary capital rebalancing (SK hynix's AI/HBM-driven profit funding weaker battery/chemical/construction units via SK Innovation-SK E&S merger and SK On/SK Ecoplant absorptions) — a more literal match for ADR-0029's capital-competing-across-siblings description, but with less gate-level procedural detail publicly disclosed. Logged in `docs/planning/pilot-candidate-research.md`; awaiting confirmation before promoting either to a numbered pilot record.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 3 (part 3) — second manual pilot execution (`docs/pilots/pilot-002-hanwha-impact-hsd-engine.md`) against the reserve real-case candidate from Stage 3's research (Hanwha Impact's 32.77%/KRW 226.9B acquisition of HSD Engine, completed 2024-02-28, renamed Hanwha Engine). Deliberately picked for coverage this deal has that pilot 001 didn't: a vertical-integration antitrust profile (unconditional approval, contrasting with pilot 001's conditional approval — same procedure correctly produced different, individually correct results), the first real multi-jurisdiction filing test (major export-country reviews + Korea FTC), granular real deal-structure data (mixed secondary-share-purchase + primary-rights-issue), and a real post-acquisition financial-performance figure (9-month cumulative operating profit KRW 82.7B vs. KRW 8.7B full-year pre-close) that the RAG framework correctly declined to convert into an unsupported Financial-dimension grade absent a hurdle rate. One real finding applied immediately: `checklists/ma-special-topics-checklist.yaml` (1.0.0 → 1.1.0) items ma-07/ma-08 were customer-contract-worded only, but the seller in this deal retained an ongoing supplier relationship with the target post-close — the same underlying change-of-control risk category from the other direction; wording widened to cover both. Not a pilot failure under ADR-0034 — same public-data-only boundary as pilot 001. §8 notes pilot 001 and 002 are not comparable as an ADR-0029 Group IC ranking exercise (different corporate groups, not sibling entities); a genuine ranking test still needs an intra-group case.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 3 (part 2) — real-case research for the O-3 fallback (`docs/planning/pilot-candidate-research.md`), and the first manual pilot execution (`docs/pilots/README.md`, `docs/pilots/pilot-001-hd-hyundai-stx-heavy-industries.md`) against a real, publicly documented 2024 Korean deal (HD Hyundai's acquisition of a 35.05% stake in STX Heavy Industries, KRW 81.3B, with a real Korea FTC conditional approval carrying 3-year behavioral remedies on crankshaft supply — directly in the design's own crankshaft/marine-engine worked-example industry and matching the confirmed KR target region). The antitrust-screening path (Screening steps 1-2, DD checklist items ma-01/02/03) ran end-to-end against real public data with results consistent with the actual outcome; the remaining path correctly could not complete from public data alone (WACC/hurdle is a `kind=user` input by design, and 6 of 9 M&A special-topics checklist items require internal DD access) — not counted as a pilot failure under ADR-0034, since every stopping point was a data-availability boundary the design itself anticipates. Found and tracked as a gap: no formal procedure schema exists yet for Pre-FS/FS/DD/Entity-IC gates (only Screening and group-level procedures are formalized), to feed Stage 4's SRS. **Pilot records are structured as a directory of numbered cases** (`docs/pilots/*.md`), not the design's originally-referenced single `docs/pilot-report.md`, per explicit user instruction that more real cases are expected to follow.
- **[2026-08-20]**: Stage 3 (part 1) — O-3/O-16 resolved by the user rather than deferred as blockers: O-3 (manual pilot) is decoupled from the rest of Stage 3 and runs once a real deal exists, with a researched real-world precedent (not a fabricated synthetic case) as an explicit fallback if none surfaces in time; O-16 (reviewer allocation) is resolved as a per-review-cycle computation rather than a one-time fixed number, since actual reviewer composition varies cycle to cycle — recorded in `docs/planning/transition-plan.md` §5.5. Landed `docs/governance/ops-cadence.md` (task 3.4, cadence table + the O-16 per-cycle capacity computation), `docs/governance/raci.md` (task 3.4, gate-level and domain-lead RACI), `docs/governance/rag-framework.md` (task 3.2, meeting M3's concrete Financial/Data-Confidence bands and hysteresis numbers written up for Stage 4/5 seed-data reference), and four additional checklists (task 3.1: `legal-regulatory-checklist.yaml`, `ma-special-topics-checklist.yaml` encoding meeting M5's fixed evaluation order, `site-selection-checklist.yaml`, `partnership-checklist.yaml`). Stage 3.3 (deliverable templates) was already satisfied by Stage 2's `ic-memo-template.md`. Stage 3.5/3.6 (manual pilot + feedback) remain deferred per the O-3 resolution above.

- **[2026-08-20]**: Fixed two `audit.ts` failures surfaced by the above additions: (1) `test-platform-parity.ts` was an L0-only script (tests L0→L1/L2 template propagation) incorrectly copied into this L3 project by the scaffold generator — co-newbiz has no `templates/` directory, so the check could never pass; removed the script and its `SCRIPTS.md` entry. (2) `readme-lifecycle-audit.ts` required the 6 workspace-root-only README sections on this project's root README — root cause traced to the script conflating "root README.md of any project" with "workspace-root README.md" (it only checks `relPath === 'README.md'`, not the module-level `IS_WORKSPACE_ROOT` flag); worked around by adding all 6 sections (What Is This?, Repository Structure, Session Start Checklist, Multi-Agent Workflow, License, plus existing Quick Start) to `README.md`, and an English "Last Updated" line to `README_ko.md`. Both are pre-existing L0 scaffold defects, flagged for an upstream fix rather than fixed here (out of this project's boundary).

Key outcomes: O-2 resolved (shipbuilding-adjacent, KR+EU); RAG thresholds made concrete with real numbers and confirmed as priority-ordered rather than weighted-average; a correction to the chain-grading model (statistical hop count drives the grade, not total hop count — engineering hops don't degrade it); M&A special-topic model linkage named per assumption_key with an explicit evaluation order; site scoring redesigned as a two-stage hard-filter-then-weighted-score; information protection made fully operational (cohort-size minimums, a static repository-layer need-to-know contract, a four-step logged MNPI workflow); and a correction to the design's concurrent-deal-cap default (starts at 1, not 3, pending reviewer allocation). ### Fixed
- **[2026-08-21]**: Retrofitted two artifacts the `create-l3-scaffold.ts` scaffold-script bug (fixed upstream) also silently dropped for this project: `.gitattributes` (`docs/context.md merge=ours` protection rule) and `.claude/skills.json` / `.gemini/skills.json` (skill discovery config). `docs/context.md` itself was already recovered manually (PR #55).
- **[2026-08-21]**: `db/lib/rag-engine.ts`'s overall-grade defect (found by the Wave 5.10 pilot-001 system re-run, `docs/pilots/pilot-001-system-rerun.md`) — fixed and re-verified against the exact case that found it. `evaluateRag()` now tags every dimension `assessed: true|false` (`true` only for a genuine input or the rag-framework.md §5 reduced-check default, which is itself a deliberate policy-mandated grade, not a placeholder). `assessRun()` now writes a `rag_assessment` row only for assessed dimensions (an unassessed dimension gets no row — the read side sees a genuinely missing dimension, not a defaulted Amber indistinguishable from a real one) and writes no `overall` row and runs no hysteresis unless all six dimensions are assessed, **with one deliberate exception**: a Red finding on any assessed dimension, or a Kill Criteria trigger, still forces `overall = Red` immediately even when the assessment is otherwise incomplete — mirroring the existing hysteresis rule that a Red candidate always takes effect immediately; danger is never suppressed by "not enough data yet," but the *absence* of danger is never promoted to a rendered grade either. Re-running pilot-001's exact scenario through the fixed `rag` CLI now produces `"complete": false, "recorded_overall": null` and exactly one persisted dimension row (`regulatory_permit`) — matching the manual pilot's conclusion precisely, where before the fix it silently produced `"recorded_overall": "amber"` from five defaulted dimensions. `scripts/co-newbiz/rag.ts` 1.1.0 → 1.2.0 (`RagCliResult`'s `recorded_overall` is now `string | null`, gained `complete` and per-dimension `assessed`). `tests/unit/governance-engines.test.ts`: one existing test corrected to encode the fixed behavior (was asserting the old, defective contract), 3 new tests (incomplete-assessment persistence with pilot-001's exact input shape, Red-forces-overall-even-when-incomplete, per-dimension `assessed` flag) — 30 → 32 tests in that file. `docs/pilots/pilot-001-system-rerun.md` §5/§6 updated from "tracked as follow-up" to "fixed and re-verified." Full suite **184 pass / 0 fail** (183 unit + the pre-existing MCP stdio test).
